67: ## 2. Task Definitions & Status
68: 
69: ### Completed MVP Slices
70: 
71: - [x] **T1 — Project Shell & Tooling Baseline**
72:   - **Contract**: Scaffold Flutter project with FVM (Flutter 3.47.2 / Dart 3.13.2) supporting mobile, desktop, and web; configure `analysis_options.yaml` and dependencies (`chess`, `uuid`, `equatable`, `clock`, `path_provider`, `shared_preferences`).
73:   - **Verification**: `flutter analyze` passes.
74: 
75: - [x] **T2 — Core Domain Model**
76:   - **Contract**: Define immutable domain entities (`Study`, `Chapter`, `PositionNode`, `PositionKey`, `RepertoireMove`, `RepertoireDecision`) with zero Flutter/third-party package dependencies.
77:   - **Verification**: Type system validation and constructor immutability checks.
78: 
79: - [x] **T3 — Chess Rules Adapter**
80:   - **Contract**: Build `ChessService` encapsulating chess rules, legal move generation, coordinate conversions, and resilient SAN resolution.
81:   - **Verification**: `test/chess_service_test.dart` (7/7 tests passed).
82: 
83: - [x] **T4 — PGN Parser with Recursive Variations**
84:   - **Contract**: Build `PgnParser` tokenizing movetext, parsing multi-game splits, headers, comments, NAGs, and recursive variation subtrees.
85:   - **Verification**: `test/pgn_parser_test.dart` (6/6 tests passed).
86: 
87: - [x] **T5 — SRS Scheduling Engine**
88:   - **Contract**: Implement `Scheduler` abstraction and `SimpleScheduler` with exponential interval growth, lapse recovery, and injectable `Clock` for deterministic testing.
89:   - **Verification**: `test/srs_scheduler_test.dart` (4/4 tests passed).
90: 
91: - [x] **T6 — PGN Import Normalization Pipeline**
92:   - **Contract**: Implement `PgnConverter` and `ImportService` to normalize parsed PGN games into complete `Study` hierarchies with position nodes and structured error reporting.
93:   - **Verification**: `test/import_service_test.dart` (4/4 tests passed).
94: 
95: - [x] **T7 — Review Application Coordinator**
96:   - **Contract**: Implement `ReviewService` and `ReviewEngine` managing due decision discovery, answer verification, auto-continuation through learned moves, and SRS state progression.
97:   - **Verification**: `test/review_service_test.dart` (2/2 tests passed).
98: 
99: - [x] **T8 — Local Persistence Store**
100:   - **Contract**: Implement `StudyRepository` interface and `InMemoryStudyRepository` storing studies, position trees, decisions, and review state/event histories.
101:   - **Verification**: Service integration tests with persistence assertions.
102: 
103: - [x] **T9 — Review-First UI Scene**
104:   - **Contract**: Implement Flutter application startup directly into the Review scene with responsive board rendering, algebraic move input, feedback display, and progression.
105:   - **Verification**: `test/widget_test.dart` smoke test.
106:

<content>
1: # Product Definition
2: 
3: ## 1. Vision & Core Value
4: 
5: **Chess Repertoire SRS** is a focused, local-first chess learning application for **memorizing and retaining opening and middlegame repertoires through active spaced repetition**.
6: 
7: The core premise:
8: > **Show the player a position from their own repertoire and ask them to play what they have learned.**
9: 
10: The application is not a general tactics trainer, an engine analysis cockpit, or a social network. Its purpose is to turn a player's curated repertoire material into **instant, reliable, retrievable memory**.
11: 
12: ---
13: 
14: ## 2. Target Users
15: 
16: - **Tournament and competitive chess players** preparing structured opening repertoires.
17: - **Club players and students** who study PGN files or coach-provided material and need to remember lines under game conditions.
18: - **Players tired of feature bloat** who want a minimal, zero-latency review utility that opens directly into active practice.
19: 
20: ---
21: 
22: ## 3. Core User Journeys
23: 
24: ### Journey 1: Daily Repertoire Review (Default)
25: 1. User opens the application.
26: 2. The app immediately presents the chessboard on the first due position across all imported studies.
27: 3. User recalls and makes their prepared move on the board.
28: 4. If correct: subtle positive feedback, automatic traversal through already-learned opponent/user continuations, and stopping at the next due decision.
29: 5. If incorrect: immediate subtle feedback showing the expected repertoire continuation, recording a failed recall, and advancing.
30: 6. User reviews as long as they wish; when all items are up to date, a calm idle state is displayed.
31: 
32: ### Journey 2: First Launch & PGN Import
33: 1. User opens the app for the very first time (zero studies exist).
34: 2. The app presents a clean, focused import interface.
35: 3. User supplies a PGN file containing one or many games/chapters with variations, comments, and annotations.
36: 4. The system validates chess legality, preserves full variation trees, normalizes chapters, and derives scheduled decision points.
37: 5. User is immediately transitioned into Review of the newly imported material.
38: 
39: ### Journey 3: Constrained Review Scope
40: 1. User is preparing for a specific match (e.g. against 1.e4) and wants to focus on their Sicilian repertoire.
41: 2. User opens the lightweight study/opening filter drawer.
42: 3. User selects the target study or opening category.
43: 4. The Review scene updates its active pool to only test due positions matching that filter.
44: 
45: ---
46: 
47: ## 4. Functional Requirements
48: 
49: 1. **PGN Pipeline**:
50:    - Accept standard and non-standard PGNs with single or multiple games/chapters.
51:    - Preserve recursive annotation variations (RAVs) as first-class branching trees.
52:    - Support arbitrary starting FEN positions.
53:    - Extract move comments and NAGs without corrupting tree structure.
54: 
55: 2. **Repertoire Training Model**:
56:    - Training evaluates whether the player remembered **their repertoire**, not whether the move is engine-optimal.
57:    - Opponent mistakes and prepared punishments are first-class repertoire content.
58:    - Player decision points are derived for the user's perspective.
59: 
60: 3. **Spaced Repetition System (SRS)**:
61:    - Every repertoire decision maintains its own SRS state (repetition count, lapse count, stability/interval, next due timestamp).
62:    - Non-due (already learned) positions are automatically traversed during review so the user is only tested on what is currently due.
63:    - Previously learned moves return as active testable questions once their interval elapses.
64:    - Scheduler algorithm is replaceable behind an abstract contract.
65: 
66: 4. **Offline & Performance**:
67:    - The entire review and move-validation loop is 100% local and requires zero network access.
68:    - Perceived interaction latency must be effectively zero (<16ms on mobile and desktop).
69: 
70: ---
71: 
72: ## 5. UX & Design Principles (Dogmatic Minimalism & Lichess Professional Polish)
73: 
74: - **The Product is Review**: The chessboard is the primary and dominant interface element, accompanied by a professional move history sidebar and study navigator.
75: - **Quiet & Fast Feedback**: Move confirmation is immediate with subtle tactile/audio feedback cues and clean status banners.
76: - **Lichess Design Standards**: Calibrated dark theme (`#161512` background, `#262421` surface, `#629924` accent), SVG chess pieces, clean algebraic notation tree with variant folding, and responsive desktop split view.
77: - **No Mandatory Session Boundary**: Review is an ongoing utility. Users can stop anytime without penalty.
78: - **Restrained Visual Aesthetic**: Subtle typography, generous negative space, high-contrast board pieces, no gamer aesthetics.
79: 
80: ---
81: 
82: ## 6. Non-Goals (Explicit Exclusions)
83: 
84: - No tactics puzzle generator or arbitrary position solver.
85: - No real-time chess engine judging review moves (engine evaluation does not dictate repertoire correctness).
86: - No multiplayer, matchmaking, or social leaderboards.
87: - No mandatory daily gamification streaks or XP badges.
88: - No cloud dependency for core move validation.
89: 
90: ---
91: 
92: ## 7. Future Horizons (Post-MVP)
93: 
94: - **Lichess Integration**: Authenticate and directly import/sync user's Lichess studies.
95: - **Cross-Device Async Sync**: Background synchronization of study content and SRS review history across mobile, web, and desktop.
96: - **Advisory Import Health Check**: Optional asynchronous one-time engine pass warning users of severe evaluation collapses in their own lines (advisory only, never blocking).
97: - **Cross-Study Opening Categorization**: Aggregate repertoire positions by opening family across separate study files.

(End of file - total 97 lines)
</content>
```

<content>
1: # MVP Scope & Specifications
2: 
3: ## 1. MVP Goal
4: 
5: Deliver a functional, local-first Flutter application (mobile, desktop, web) that executes the complete loop:
6: **PGN Import → Normalized Repertoire Tree → Active Review Scene → Local SRS Persistence**.
7: 
8: ---
9: 
10: ## 2. Included Capabilities
11: 
12: | Capability | Description | Status |
13: |---|---|---|
14: | **Local PGN Import** | Multi-game, multi-chapter PGN parser supporting recursive variations (RAVs), comments, NAGs, and starting FENs. | Complete |
15: | **Repertoire Normalization** | Converts PGN trees into domain `Study` → `Chapter` → `PositionNode` graphs with deterministic FEN keys. | Complete |
16: | **Decision Point Derivation** | Derives `RepertoireDecision` units for positions requiring user recall. | Complete |
17: | **Active Recall Review** | Interactive chessboard scene where users input moves to answer due repertoire positions. | Complete |
18: | **Move Validation** | Immediate local validation against expected repertoire continuation moves. | Complete |
19: | **Automatic Traversal** | Automated traversal through opponent replies and already-learned user moves until the next due decision. | Complete |
20: | **Replaceable SRS Scheduler** | `Scheduler` contract + `SimpleScheduler` exponential interval ladder with lapse recovery and deterministic testing support. | Complete |
21: | **Local Persistence** | `StudyRepository` interface with `InMemoryStudyRepository` and file-backed persistence. | Complete |
22: | **Cross-Platform Support** | Flutter codebase supporting Linux, macOS, Windows, Android, iOS, and Web. | Complete |
23: 
24: ---
25: 
26: ## 3. Excluded Capabilities (Deferred to Later Phases)
27: 
28: ```text
29: PRODUCT SCOPE
30: ├── Local PGN Import               [MVP]
31: ├── Recursive Variation Trees      [MVP]
32: ├── Review-First Active Recall     [MVP]
33: ├── Automatic Traversal            [MVP]
34: ├── Replaceable SRS Engine         [MVP]
35: ├── Local Persistence              [MVP]
36: ├── Advisory Engine Health Check   [Phase 2]
37: ├── Cross-Study Opening Filter     [Phase 2]
38: ├── Lichess OAuth & Study Sync     [Phase 3]
39: ├── Cross-Device Cloud Sync        [Phase 4]
40: └── Tactics / Social / Gamify      [EXCLUDED]
41: ```
42: 
43: ---
44: 
45: ## 4. Acceptance Criteria
46: 
47: 1. **Clean Import**: Importing a PGN containing multiple chapters and nested variations produces a fully intact hierarchy where no variation branch is discarded.
48: 2. **Deterministic Correctness**: Playing a move that matches an active repertoire branch is accepted immediately; playing any other move (even legal chess moves) is marked incorrect.
49: 3. **SRS Interval Progression**:
50:    - New items are due immediately upon creation.
51:    - Successful recalls increase the interval (1 day → 2 days → 4 days → ...).
52:    - Failed recalls reset the repetition streak and schedule a short-interval retry.
53: 4. **Zero-Lag Interaction**: Move legality and repertoire lookup happen synchronously in memory without blocking UI rendering.
54: 5. **No Modal Interruptions**: Incorrect moves reveal the expected line and allow progression without requiring modal dismissals.
55: 6. **Persistence Durability**: Review states and study structures survive application restarts.
56: 
57: ---
58: 
59: ## 5. Known Limitations (MVP)
60: 
61: - Default player orientation heuristic is White unless specified in chapter metadata or custom FEN.
62: - UI uses clean Unicode chess glyphs on a responsive board grid; custom SVG vector sets will be refined in UI polishing iterations.

(End of file - total 62 lines)
</content>
```

<type>file</type>
<content>
1: # Technical Architecture
2: 
3: ## 1. Architectural Style
4: 
5: Chess Repertoire SRS is built as a **local-first, layered, modular system** designed for zero perceived latency during review and forward-compatibility with asynchronous cloud synchronization.
6: 
7: ---
8: 
9: ## 2. Layer Hierarchy & Boundaries
10: 
11: ```text
12: ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
13: │                   Presentation / UI                    │
14: │            (Flutter Widgets, Board, Prompts)           │
15: └───────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
16:                             │
17: ┌───────────────────────────▼────────────────────────────┐
18: │                   Application Layer                    │
19: │             (ReviewService, ImportService)             │
20: └─────────────┬────────────────────────────┬─────────────┘
21:               │                            │
22: ┌─────────────▼──────────────┐ ┌───────────▼─────────────┐
23: │        Domain Layer        │ │       Chess Layer       │
24: │  (Study, Chapter, Tree,    │ │ (ChessService, Parser,  │
25: │   Decisions, SRS States)   │ │  PGN Normalization)     │
26: └─────────────▲──────────────┘ └───────────▲─────────────┘
27:               │                            │
28: ┌─────────────┴────────────────────────────┴─────────────┐
29: │                   Persistence Layer                    │
30: │      (StudyRepository, InMemory, Local File Store)     │
31: └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
32: ```
33: 
34: ### Boundary Invariants
35: 
36: 1. **Pure Domain**: The domain layer (`lib/domain/`) contains pure Dart logic and entity models. It has **zero dependencies** on Flutter UI packages (`flutter/material.dart`, widgets) and zero dependencies on database engines or external chess package internals.
37: 2. **Encapsulated Chess Engine**: External chess rule packages (e.g. `package:chess`) are strictly encapsulated within `lib/chess/chess_service.dart`. Domain entities and UI components interact only via normalized value objects (`PositionKey`, `RepertoireMove`, `ChessMove`).
38: 3. **Local-First Critical Path**: The move-validation loop (`user move -> legality -> repertoire match -> SRS update -> board update`) is executed completely in memory and synchronously saved locally. Network access is never placed on this path.
39: 
40: ---
41: 
42: ## 3. Component Responsibilities
43: 
44: ### Domain Layer (`lib/domain/`)
45: - **`Study` & `Chapter`**: Root content containers representing imported repertoire collections and individual chapters/lines.
46: - **`PositionNode`**: Nodes in the position tree containing normalized FEN keys, incoming moves, annotations, comments, and recursive child branches.
47: - **`RepertoireDecision`**: The primary learning unit representing a position where the player must recall their prepared repertoire move.
48: - **`ReviewState` & `ReviewEvent`**: Spaced repetition tracking (streak, lapses, stability, next due timestamp) and immutable review logs.
49: - **`Scheduler` & `SimpleScheduler`**: Contract and default interval ladder for computing SRS transitions.
50: - **`Clock`**: Time abstraction enabling deterministic testing of time-dependent review intervals.
51: 
52: ### Chess Layer (`lib/chess/`)
53: - **`ChessService`**: Adapter wrapping the rules engine. Performs SAN resolution, legal move generation, coordinate conversions (from/to/promotion), and FEN generation without leaking package types.
54: - **`PgnParser`**: Tokenizer and recursive-descent parser for raw PGN movetext supporting multi-game files, comments, NAGs, and recursive variations (RAV).
55: - **`PgnConverter`**: Walks parsed PGN ASTs with `ChessService` to produce validated `PositionNode` trees.
56: 
57: ### Import Layer (`lib/import/`)
58: - **`ImportService`**: Orchestrates raw PGN splitting, parsing, tree normalization, and chapter generation with detailed error/warning accumulation.
59: 
60: ### Persistence Layer (`lib/persistence/`)
61: - **`StudyRepository`**: Interface defining contracts for persisting studies, chapters, position trees, decisions, review states, and review events.
62: - **`InMemoryStudyRepository`**: Fast in-memory implementation for test suites and initial state management.
63: 
64: ### Application Layer (`lib/application/`)
65: - **`ReviewService`**: Application coordinator that queries due decisions across studies, validates submitted user moves, updates SRS records, and determines next positions.
66: 
67: ---
68: 
69: ## 4. Entity Identity & Position Model
70: 
71: - **Stable Entity IDs**: All entities (`Study`, `Chapter`, `PositionNode`, `RepertoireDecision`, `ReviewState`) use UUID v4 strings for globally unique, sync-ready identification.
72: - **Position Key Normalization**: Position keys are derived from normalized 4-field FEN segments (piece placement, side-to-move, castling rights, and en-passant square) ensuring full chess state fidelity.
73: 
74: ---
75: 
76: ## 5. Review Engine State Machine
77: 
78: ```text
79:                ┌───────────────┐
80:                │ Select Scope  │
81:                └───────┬───────┘
82:                        │
83:                        ▼
84:                ┌───────────────┐
85:                │ Due Decision? ├─── No ───► Show Idle Screen
86:                └───────┬───────┘
87:                        │ Yes
88:                        ▼
89:                ┌───────────────┐
90:                │ Show Position │
91:                └───────┬───────┘
92:                        │
93:                        ▼
94:                ┌───────────────┐
95:                │ User Plays SAN│
96:                └───────┬───────┘
97:                        │
98:          ┌─────────────┴─────────────┐
99:          ▼                           ▼
100:    [Correct Move]             [Incorrect Move]

<content>
1: # Quality & Engineering Invariants
2: 
3: This document defines the **non-negotiable properties, constraints, and invariants** that must be maintained across all implementations, refactorings, and feature additions in Chess Repertoire SRS.
4: 
5: ---
6: 
7: ## 1. Architectural Invariants
8: 
9: 1. **Layer Independence**:
10:    - The Presentation layer (`lib/ui/`, `lib/main.dart`) must never directly interact with database tables, raw storage blobs, or external chess package internals.
11:    - The Domain layer (`lib/domain/`) must remain pure Dart with **zero imports** of Flutter widget libraries (`package:flutter/...`) or third-party chess package types (`package:chess/...`).
12: 2. **Encapsulation of Third-Party Chess Types**:
13:    - Package-specific types (e.g. `ch.Chess`, `ch.Move`, `ch.Piece`) must be strictly confined to `lib/chess/`. They must never appear in domain entity signatures or application service return types.
14: 3. **Local-First Zero-Latency Review**:
15:    - The critical review loop (`user move submission -> move validation -> SRS update -> board update`) must execute entirely in memory and locally.
16:    - Network calls are strictly forbidden on the critical path of move submission and board rendering.
17: 4. **Advisory Engine Isolation**:
18:    - If an engine analysis pass is introduced (e.g. at import), it must run asynchronously outside the review loop and remain purely advisory. An engine evaluation must never veto or block the user's repertoire import.
19: 
20: ---
21: 
22: ## 2. Domain & Chess Invariants
23: 
24: 1. **Repertoire Correctness over Engine Truth**:
25:    - Correctness during Review is strictly defined by the user's repertoire material, not engine evaluation.
26:    - Opponent inaccuracies and mistakes are valid repertoire content (intended to teach prepared punishments).
27: 2. **Variation Preservation**:
28:    - PGN import must preserve recursive annotation variations (RAVs) as first-class sibling branches under their respective parent nodes.
29:    - Variations must never be flattened or silently deleted during parsing or conversion.
30: 3. **Position Identity Integrity**:
31:    - A position's identity must account for piece placement, active side to move, castling availability, and en-passant target square.
32: 4. **Long-Term Retrieval (No Permanent Exclusion)**:
33:    - A learned move that is auto-traversed during non-due intervals is **never permanently excluded** from review. It must become an active question again once its scheduled due date arrives.
34: 
35: ---
36: 
37: ## 3. Reliability & Error Invariants
38: 
39: 1. **Graceful Degradation over Silent Corruption**:
40:    - Malformed PGN moves or invalid headers must be quarantined with structured error messages (containing chapter title and move index) rather than crashing the importer or silently creating broken position graphs.
41: 2. **Deterministic Time Testing**:
42:    - All time-dependent domain logic (SRS scheduling, due item queries) must accept an abstract `Clock` instance so test suites can run deterministically without depending on system wall-clock time.
43: 
44: ---
45: 
46: ## 4. Performance Invariants
47: 
48: 1. **Move Response Time**: Local move legality validation and repertoire lookup must complete in **< 16 milliseconds** (within a single display frame).
49: 2. **Incremental State Writes**: Review state updates must be persisted incrementally without rewriting the entire study tree on every move.

(End of file - total 49 lines)
</content>
```

18: | **Review-First UI Rendering** | Widget smoke tests (`test/widget_test.dart`) | `ChessRepertoireApp` |

30: ├── srs_scheduler_test.dart    # Fixed clock tests for initial due, interval scaling, lapse streak resets

33: ```

37: ## 3. Verification Levels & The Quality Gate
38:

43: ```
44: 
45: ### Level 2: Full Local Quality Gate (`./verify`)
46: Before committing or marking a task complete, run the mandatory local quality gate:
47: ```bash
48: ./verify
49: ```

51: 1. `flutter analyze` (Static type-checking, linter compliance, zero warnings).
52: 2. `flutter test` (Full automated test suite execution).
53: 
54: ### Level 3: Continuous Integration (CI)
55: On every pull request and push to `main`, GitHub Actions runs `./verify` in a clean environment to ensure zero regression across platforms.

49:     StudyRepository repo;

52:       if (Platform.isLinux || Platform.isMacOS || Platform.isWindows) {
53:         final docDir = await getApplicationDocumentsDirectory();

68:       scheduler: const SimpleScheduler(),
69:       clock: _clock,
70:     );
71: 
72:     // Seed starter repertoire only if empty (non-blocking)
73:     final existingStudies = await repo.getAllStudies();
74:     if (existingStudies.isEmpty) {
75:       await _seedStarterRepertoire(repo);

86:   Future<void> _seedStarterRepertoire(StudyRepository repo) async {
87:     const defaultPgn = '''
88: [Event "Italian Game: Main Line"]
89: [Site "Chess Study"]
90: 
91: 1. e4 e5 2. Nf3 Nc6 3. Bc4 Bc5 4. c3 Nf6 5. d3 *
92: 
93: [Event "Sicilian Defense: Open"]
94: [Site "Chess Study"]
95:

101:   Future<void> _importPgnWithRepo(StudyRepository repo, String pgnText, String title) async {
102:     final result = _importService.importPgn(pgnText, studyTitle: title);
103:     if (result.study.id.isNotEmpty) {
104:       await repo.saveStudy(result.study);
105:       for (final chapter in result.chapters) {
106:         await repo.saveChapter(chapter);
107:         if (chapter.root != null) {
108:           await repo.savePositionTree(chapter.id, chapter.root!);
109:           _saveDecisionsRecursive(repo, result.study.id, chapter.id, chapter.root!);
110:         }
111:       }
112:     }
113:   }
114: 
115:   void _saveDecisionsRecursive(StudyRepository repo, String studyId, String chapterId, PositionNode node) {
116:     if (!node.isLeaf && node.childMoves.isNotEmpty) {
117:       final decision = RepertoireDecision.create(
118:         studyId: studyId,
119:         chapterId: chapterId,
120:         nodeId: node.id,
121:         expectedMoves: node.childMoves,
122:       );
123:       repo.saveDecision(decision);
124:     }
125:     for (final child in node.children) {
126:       _saveDecisionsRecursive(repo, studyId, chapterId, child);
127:     }
128:   }

224:   Future<void> _loadInitialDecision() async {
225:     setState(() => _isLoading = true);
226:     _allStudies = await widget.repository.getAllStudies();
227: 
228:     // Compute due counts per study
229:     final counts = <String, int>{};
230:     for (final study in _allStudies) {
231:       final studyDue = await widget.reviewService.getDueDecisions(studyId: study.id);
232:       counts[study.id] = studyDue.length;
233:     }
234:     _studyDueCounts = counts;
235: 
236:     final decisions = await widget.reviewService.getDueDecisions(studyId: _scopedStudyId);
237:     _dueCount = decisions.length;
238: 
239:     if (decisions.isNotEmpty) {
240:       final decision = decisions.first;
241:       _currentDecision = decision;
242:       final chapter = await widget.repository.getChapter(decision.chapterId);
243:       if (chapter != null && chapter.root != null) {
244:         _currentNode = _findNodeById(chapter.root!, decision.nodeId);
245:       }
246:     } else {
247:       _currentDecision = null;
248:       _currentNode = null;

259:   PositionNode? _findNodeById(PositionNode root, String targetId) {
260:     if (root.id == targetId) return root;
261:     for (final child in root.children) {
262:       final found = _findNodeById(child, targetId);
263:       if (found != null) return found;
264:     }
265:     return null;

268:   void _onSquareTapped(String square) {
269:     if (_currentDecision == null || _currentNode == null) return;
270: 
271:     if (_selectedSquare == null) {
272:       setState(() {
273:         _selectedSquare = square;
274:       });
275:     } else {
276:       final from = _selectedSquare!;
277:       final to = square;
278:       setState(() {
279:         _selectedSquare = null;
280:       });

287:   Future<void> _submitMove(String from, String to, String? promotion) async {
288:     if (_currentDecision == null || _currentNode == null) return;
289: 
290:     final result = await widget.reviewService.processAnswer(
291:       currentNode: _currentNode!,

297:

303:         _feedback = '✓ Correct';
304:         _expectedMove = null;
305:       });
306:       
307:       // Handle automatic continuation

310:         await Future.delayed(const Duration(milliseconds: 500));
311:       }
312:       
313:       if (continuation?.nextPrompt != null) {
314:         // There's a next due decision - load it

316:         final chapter = await widget.repository.getChapter(

327:         }

330:           _selectedSquare = null;
331:         });
332:       } else {
333:         // No more due decisions - reload

336:     } else {

342:       // Still process the review result for SRS

344:       
345:       // After showing error, auto-continue to next due decision

531:                             ? 'No positions currently due for this study.'

555:                             color: _feedback.startsWith('✓') ? const Color(0xFF81B64C) : const Color(0xFFE06C75),
556:                           ),

562:                         child: Text(

564:                           style: const TextStyle(color: Colors.amber, fontStyle: FontStyle.italic),
565:                         ),

573:                             child: _buildBoard(),
574:                           ),

580:                       child: Text(
581:                         'Tap piece to select, tap square to move',
582:                         style: TextStyle(color: Colors.grey),
583:                       ),
584:                     ),
585:                   ],
586:                 ),
587:     );
588:   }
589: 
590:   Widget _buildBoard() {
591:     final fen = _currentNode?.fen ?? ChessService.initialFen;
592:     final board = _getBoardAsStrings(fen);
593: 
594:     return Container(
595:       decoration: BoxDecoration(
596:         border: Border.all(color: Colors.black45, width: 2),
597:       ),
598:       child: GridView.builder(
599:         physics: const NeverScrollableScrollPhysics(),
600:         gridDelegate: const SliverGridDelegateWithFixedCrossAxisCount(
601:           crossAxisCount: 8,
602:         ),
603:         itemCount: 64,
604:         itemBuilder: (context, index) {
605:           final row = index ~/ 8;
606:           final col = index % 8;
607:           final isDark = (row + col) % 2 == 1;
608: 
609:           // Rank is 8 - row, file is a + col
610:           final rank = 8 - row;
611:           final file = String.fromCharCode('a'.codeUnitAt(0) + col);
612:           final square = '$file$rank';
613:           final isSelected = _selectedSquare == square;
614:           final piece = board[row][col];
615: 
616:           return GestureDetector(
617:             onTap: () => _onSquareTapped(square),
618:             child: Container(
619:               color: isSelected
620:                   ? const Color(0xFFBACA44)
621:                   : isDark
622:                       ? const Color(0xFF769656)
623:                       : const Color(0xFFEEEED2),
624:               alignment: Alignment.center,
625:               child: Text(
626:                 piece,
627:                 style: TextStyle(
628:                   fontSize: 34,
629:                   color: _isWhitePiece(piece) ? Colors.white : Colors.black,
630:                   shadows: [
631:                     Shadow(
632:                       color: _isWhitePiece(piece) ? Colors.black87 : Colors.white70,
633:                       blurRadius: 1.5,
634:                     ),
635:                   ],
636:                 ),
637:               ),
638:             ),
639:           );
640:         },
641:       ),
642:     );
643:   }
644: 
645:   bool _isWhitePiece(String piece) {
646:     return '♙♘♗♖♕♔'.contains(piece);
647:   }
648: 
649:   List<List<String>> _getBoardAsStrings(String fen) {
650:     final rows = fen.trim().split(' ')[0].split('/');
651:     final grid = <List<String>>[];
652:     for (final row in rows) {
653:       final rank = <String>[];
654:       for (var i = 0; i < row.length; i++) {
655:         final char = row[i];
656:         if (RegExp(r'[1-8]').hasMatch(char)) {
657:           final count = int.parse(char);
658:           for (var j = 0; j < count; j++) {
659:             rank.add('');
660:           }
661:         } else {
662:           switch (char) {
663:             case 'P': rank.add('♙'); break;
664:             case 'N': rank.add('♘'); break;
665:             case 'B': rank.add('♗'); break;
666:             case 'R': rank.add('♖'); break;
667:             case 'Q': rank.add('♕'); break;
668:             case 'K': rank.add('♔'); break;
669:             case 'p': rank.add('♟'); break;
670:             case 'n': rank.add('♞'); break;
671:             case 'b': rank.add('♝'); break;
672:             case 'r': rank.add('♜'); break;
673:             case 'q': rank.add('♛'); break;
674:             case 'k': rank.add('♚'); break;
675:             default: rank.add(''); break;
676:           }
677:         }
678:       }
679:       grid.add(rank);
680:     }
681:     return grid;
682:   }
683: }

</content>

51:   Widget _buildBoard({required double squareSize}) {

53:         ? List.generate(8, (i) => 7 - i) // 7,6,5,4,3,2,1,0 (white at bottom)
54:         : List.generate(8, (i) => i); // 0,1,2,3,4,5,6,7 (black at bottom)
55: 
56:     final files = perspective == 'white'
57:         ? List.generate(8, (i) => i) // a,b,c,d,e,f,g,h (left to right for white)
58:         : List.generate(8, (i) => 7 - i); // h,g,f,e,d,c,b,a (left to right for black)
59: 
60:     return Column(
61:       mainAxisSize: MainAxisSize.min,
62:       children: ranks.map<Widget>((rank) {
63:         final files = perspective == 'white'
64:             ? List.generate(8, (i) => i)
65:             : List.generate(8, (i) => 7 - i);
66: 
67:         return Row(
68:           mainAxisSize: MainAxisSize.min,
69:           children: files.map<Widget>((file) {

71:             final piece = game.get(square);

73:             return ChessSquare(
74:               key: ValueKey(square),
75:               x: file,
76:               y: rank,
77:               pieceType: piece?.type.shift,
78:               pieceColor: piece?.color,
79:               game: chess.Chess()..load(fen), // Fresh instance per square
80:               size: squareSize,

82:               selectedSquare: selectedSquare,

86:             );

6: /// Maps our internal piece representation to asset paths
7: class PieceImages {
8:   static const Map<int, String> _whitePieceNames = {
9:     0: 'whiteKing',     // chess.PieceType.king
10:     1: 'whiteQueen',    // chess.PieceType.queen
11:     2: 'whiteRook',     // chess.PieceType.rook
12:     3: 'whiteBishop',   // chess.PieceType.bishop
13:     4: 'whiteKnight',   // chess.PieceType.knight
14:     5: 'whitePawn',     // chess.PieceType.pawn
15:   };
16: 
17:   static const Map<int, String> _blackPieceNames = {
18:     0: 'blackKing',     // chess.PieceType.king
19:     1: 'blackQueen',    // chess.PieceType.queen
20:     2: 'blackRook',     // chess.PieceType.rook
21:     3: 'blackBishop',   // chess.PieceType.bishop
22:     4: 'blackKnight',   // chess.PieceType.knight
23:     5: 'blackPawn',     // chess.PieceType.pawn
24:   };
25: 
26:   /// Returns the asset name (without extension) for a given piece type and color
27:   static String pieceName(int type, chess.Color color) {
28:     final names = color == chess.Color.WHITE ? _whitePieceNames : _blackPieceNames;
29:     return names[type]!;
30:   }
31: 
32:   /// Returns the full asset path for a piece
33:   static String assetPath(int type, chess.Color color) {
34:     return 'assets/pieces/${pieceName(type, color)}.svg';
35:   }
36: 
37:   /// Returns the asset path for a piece given by our internal RepertoireMove
38:   static String assetPathFromMove({
39:     required String pieceType, // 'k', 'q', 'r', 'b', 'n', 'p'
40:     required String color, // 'w' or 'b'
41:   }) {
42:     final pieceMap = <String, String>{
43:       'k': 'king',
44:       'q': 'queen',
45:       'r': 'rook',
46:       'b': 'bishop',
47:       'n': 'knight',
48:       'p': 'pawn',
49:     };
50:     final colorPrefix = color == 'w' ? 'white' : 'black';
51:     final pieceName = pieceMap[pieceType.toLowerCase()]!;
52:     return 'assets/pieces/${colorPrefix}${pieceName[0].toUpperCase()}${pieceName.substring(1)}.svg';
53:   }
54: 
55:   /// Preloads all piece images for faster rendering
56:   static Future<void> preloadAll(AssetBundle bundle) async {
57:     const pieceNames = [
58:       'whiteKing', 'whiteQueen', 'whiteRook', 'whiteBishop', 'whiteKnight', 'whitePawn',
59:       'blackKing', 'blackQueen', 'blackRook', 'blackBishop', 'blackKnight', 'blackPawn',
60:     ];
61:     for (final name in pieceNames) {
62:       await bundle.load('assets/pieces/$name.svg');
63:     }
64:   }
65: }

### Phase 0: Inspection Report

#### A. Which widget currently owns board rendering?
`ReviewPage` in `lib/main.dart` implements `_buildBoard()`, which renders a custom 8x8 `GridView.builder` with Unicode piece characters. (Note: There are also unused/dormant component widgets in `lib/ui/board/chessboard.dart`, `board.dart`, `board_square.dart`, `coordinates.dart`, and `piece_images.dart` referencing `.svg` assets in `assets/pieces/`, but `ReviewPage` currently directly uses its own custom inline unicode grid).

#### B. Which widget owns board state?
`_ReviewPageState` in `lib/main.dart`. It tracks `_currentNode` (which holds the current FEN string) and `_selectedSquare`.

#### C. How does the current UI obtain the chess position?
From `_currentNode?.fen` where `_currentNode` is a `PositionNode` fetched via `ReviewService` and the study repository.

#### D. How are moves currently submitted?
Via square taps on the chessboard (`_onSquareTapped` -> `_submitMove`), which takes `from` and `to` coordinates (e.g., `e2` to `e4`) and sends them to `ReviewService.processAnswer`.

#### E. Where does move validation happen?
In `ReviewService` and `ReviewEngine` (`lib/application/review_service.dart` and `lib/domain/review_engine.dart`), checking against the expected repertoire moves.

#### F. How is orientation represented?
Currently implicitly White at the bottom in `ReviewPage`, while `lib/ui/board/chessboard.dart` has a `perspective` parameter (`'white'` or `'black'`).

#### G. How are coordinates represented?
Standard algebraic strings like `"e2"`, `"e4"`, etc., and file/rank indices (`0-7`).

#### H. How are pieces currently rendered?
As Unicode characters (`♙`, `♘`, `♗`, `♖`, `♕`, `♔`, `♟`, `♞`, `♝`, `♜`, `♛`, `♚`) inside text widgets with text shadows. (SVG asset files exist under `assets/pieces/` but are not yet registered in `pubspec.yaml` or hooked up to the active board render method).

#### I. Which tests protect these behaviors?
* `test/chess_service_test.dart`
* `test/pgn_parser_test.dart`
* `test/import_service_test.dart`
* `test/srs_scheduler_test.dart`
* `test/review_service_test.dart`
* `test/local_study_repository_test.dart`
* `test/widget_test.dart`

#### J. What is the smallest possible change for T15.1?
The smallest possible change for T15.1 is to register `assets/pieces/` in `pubspec.yaml` and update `_ReviewPageState._buildBoard()` (or use `Chessboard` from `lib/ui/board/chessboard.dart`) to render the existing SVG piece assets instead of Unicode characters, preserving all existing move submission, state management, and review flow.

---
### Proposed T15.1 File Changes
1. `pubspec.yaml`: Enable `flutter.assets` and add `assets/pieces/`.
2. `lib/main.dart`: Refactor `_ReviewPageState._buildBoard()` to use SVG pieces (via `PieceImages` or `Image.asset`) corresponding to the FEN parsed from `_currentNode?.fen` using `ChessService` or `chess` package, while keeping `_onSquareTapped` and `_submitMove` 100% intact.

### Files that must NOT be touched
* Domain models (`lib/domain/**`)
* Application services (`lib/application/**`)
* Persistence (`lib/persistence/**`)
* Pgn parser / conversion (`lib/chess/**`)
* All test files

### Risks
* Missing asset bundle registration in `pubspec.yaml` causing asset loading exceptions.
* FEN parsing mismatches if a new board widget is swapped in prematurely. (Mitigation: keep `_ReviewPageState` state and interaction logic identical, only upgrading the visual piece rendering layer).

### Verification Strategy
1. Run `flutter test`.
2. Run `./verify`.

# T15.1B — Improve board geometry / responsive square scaling

Do not implement any other T15 task.

==================================================
OBJECTIVE
=========

Improve the active review board's layout so that the chessboard:

* is always perfectly square
* scales naturally with available space
* works well on desktop, tablet, and mobile
* does not distort pieces
* does not overflow its parent
* does not waste large amounts of usable screen space
* remains visually dominant in the review scene

The board should behave like a mature chess application rather than a fixed-size 8x8 grid.

==================================================
FIRST: INSPECT, DO NOT EDIT
===========================

Before changing anything:

Inspect:

* current `_buildBoard()`
* its parent layout in `ReviewPage`
* surrounding widgets
* current width/height constraints
* any `Expanded`, `Flexible`, `SizedBox`, `Container`, `GridView`, `AspectRatio`, etc.
* existing desktop/mobile behavior

Determine why the current board sizing is limited or awkward.

Do not start by rewriting the board.

==================================================
IMPLEMENTATION BOUNDARY
=======================

The intended scope is board geometry.

You may modify:

* the active board layout in `lib/main.dart`
* a very small supporting UI file only if truly necessary

Do NOT modify:

* domain
* application services
* persistence
* chess logic
* review logic
* SRS logic
* import logic
* PGN logic

Do NOT activate/migrate the dormant `lib/ui/board/chessboard.dart` system yet.

==================================================
BEHAVIOR TO PRESERVE
====================

Preserve completely:

* `_currentNode`
* `_selectedSquare`
* square mapping
* `_onSquareTapped`
* `_submitMove`
* existing FEN interpretation
* existing piece placement
* existing move submission
* review validation
* SRS progression
* auto-continuation

This task changes layout, not behavior.

==================================================
TARGET GEOMETRY
===============

The board should derive its size from the constraints available from its parent.

Conceptually:

available width + available height
↓
determine max square
↓
board = 8 × square

```

Avoid hard-coded board dimensions.

The board must remain 1:1.

Do not independently calculate width and height in ways that could create a rectangle.

==================================================
RESPONSIVE EXPECTATIONS
==================================================

Check at least these conceptual cases:

1. Narrow phone portrait
2. Phone landscape
3. Tablet
4. Typical desktop window
5. Very wide desktop window
6. Small desktop window

The board should remain usable in all cases.

On wide layouts, do not stretch the board indefinitely just because more horizontal space exists.

The board should have a sensible maximum size if necessary.

On narrow layouts, the board should shrink rather than overflow.

==================================================
IMPORTANT: DO NOT REDESIGN THE SCREEN
==================================================

Do NOT use this task as an opportunity to redesign:

- headers
- buttons
- feedback
- navigation
- margins everywhere
- typography
- colors
- review controls
- drawers
- study UI

Only solve board geometry.

If the surrounding screen layout is preventing correct board sizing, make the smallest structural adjustment necessary to establish proper constraints.

==================================================
AVOID PREMATURE ABSTRACTION
==================================================

Do not create a large responsive-layout framework.

Do not introduce a new design system.

Do not create numerous helper classes.

Use Flutter's existing layout primitives when possible.

A small, clear implementation is preferred.

==================================================
TESTING
==================================================

Add or update only meaningful tests.

Good candidates:

- board remains square under different constraints
- board fits within its parent
- orientation does not change geometry correctness
- pieces remain centered/scaled correctly

Do not create brittle pixel-perfect screenshot tests.

Do not change unrelated tests.

==================================================
VERIFICATION PROCESS
==================================================

Work incrementally.

FIRST:

make the smallest geometry change.

THEN:

run targeted tests.

THEN:

run:

./verify

If verification fails:

STOP.

Fix the problem before doing anything else.

Do not continue to another UI feature while verification is failing.

==================================================
VISUAL SELF-CHECK
==================================================

After implementation, inspect the resulting layout carefully.

Ask:

- Is the board actually square?
- Does it fit without clipping?
- Is it unnecessarily tiny?
- Is it unnecessarily huge?
- Does it remain centered/appropriately positioned?
- Does it behave sensibly when the window changes size?
- Do coordinates still fit?
- Do SVG pieces still have appropriate scale?
- Did the board accidentally affect move hitboxes?

Do not solve unrelated visual issues discovered during this inspection.

==================================================
STOP CONDITION
==================================================

When T15.1B is working and:

    ./verify

passes,

STOP.

Do not continue to T15.1C.

Report:

STEP COMPLETED:
T15.1B — Improve board geometry / responsive square scaling

FILES CHANGED:
<exact list>

BEHAVIOR CHANGED:
<yes/no; explain>

TESTS:
<exact tests/results>

VERIFY:
<exact result>

REGRESSION CHECK:
<brief confirmation>

NEXT SAFE BRICK:
T15.1C — <one sentence only>

Then stop.

The sequence is now doing what we want:

**A:** pieces → verify  
**B:** geometry → verify  
**C:** highlights/visual state → verify  
**D:** interaction controller → verify  
**E:** animation → verify

That is much safer than asking Gemini to "make the board Lichess-like."

# T15.1C — Add visual board highlights

==================================================
OBJECTIVE
=========

Improve the active review board's visual clarity by adding restrained chessboard highlights for:

1. selected square
2. last move

This is a VISUAL-STATE task only.

Do not implement interaction changes.

Do not implement animation.

Do not redesign feedback.

Do not add legal-move indicators.

Do not change review logic.

==================================================
STEP 1 — INSPECT FIRST
======================

Before editing:

Inspect the current board implementation and determine:

* how `_selectedSquare` is currently represented
* how the active board determines which squares are rendered
* whether the current move/review result contains enough information to identify the last move
* whether last-move information can be derived locally without modifying application/domain logic

Prefer using state that already exists.

Do NOT introduce a new application/domain model merely to support highlighting.

==================================================
SELECTED SQUARE
===============

The currently selected square should have a subtle visible highlight.

Requirements:

* clearly distinguish selected square
* remain readable under a piece
* do not obscure the piece
* visually fit the existing board
* use restrained opacity
* do not introduce flashy effects

Use the existing `_selectedSquare`.

Do not change how square selection works.

==================================================
LAST MOVE
=========

Add visual highlighting for the previous move when that information is already available locally.

The highlight should cover:

* source square
* destination square

It should be subtle and visually recognizable.

IMPORTANT:

If the current architecture does NOT already expose reliable last-move information to the active board, do NOT modify ReviewService/domain logic just to manufacture it.

In that case:

* document the limitation
* implement selected-square highlighting only
* stop at that safe boundary

Do not invent a second move-history system inside the widget.

==================================================
LAYERING
========

Highlights must appear beneath the chess piece but above the base square.

Conceptually:

square background
↓
highlight
↓
piece
↓
coordinate overlay if applicable

Do not allow highlights to alter board geometry.

==================================================
ORIENTATION
===========

Highlights must follow the same coordinate mapping as the pieces.

Test both conceptual orientations if orientation support already exists.

Do not redesign orientation handling in this task.

==================================================
VISUAL STYLE
============

Follow the restrained Lichess-like visual language:

* subtle
* high visibility
* not neon
* no gradients unless already part of the existing visual system
* no animation
* no giant borders
* no shadows around entire squares

The board should remain calm and focused.

==================================================
COMPONENT DESIGN
================

Keep the implementation small.

If a tiny helper/widget makes the layering clearer, that is acceptable.

Do NOT build a generic "board visual state framework."

Do NOT create abstractions for hypothetical future features.

==================================================
PRESERVE
========

Do not change:

* `_onSquareTapped`
* `_submitMove`
* ReviewService
* ReviewEngine
* Scheduler
* repository behavior
* FEN interpretation
* piece selection
* responsive sizing

==================================================
TESTING
=======

Add only useful deterministic tests.

Potential tests:

* selected square receives the expected visual state
* unselected squares do not
* both source/destination receive last-move state when reliable last-move data exists
* highlight mapping remains correct under board orientation

Avoid brittle screenshot tests.

Do not modify existing tests merely to make them pass.

==================================================
VERIFICATION
============

Work incrementally.

Make the smallest safe change.

Then:

1. run targeted tests
2. run:

   ./verify

If ./verify fails:

STOP.

Fix the failure before doing anything else.

Do not continue to another UI task.

==================================================
REGRESSION CHECK
================

Confirm:

* square tapping still works
* selected-square behavior still works
* move submission is unchanged
* review validation is unchanged
* SRS updates are unchanged
* auto-continuation is unchanged
* responsive board geometry is unchanged
* SVG pieces remain correctly positioned

==================================================
STOP CONDITION
==============

Once the visual highlight implementation is complete and ./verify passes:

STOP.

Do not implement T15.1D.

Do not add animation.

Do not add legal-move dots.

Do not add feedback animations.

Do not redesign the review screen.

Report:

657:       case '♕': return PieceImages.assetPath(1, chess.Color.WHITE);

669:   List<List<String>> _getBoardAsStrings(String fen) {
670:     final rows = fen.trim().split(' ')[0].split('/');
671:     final grid = <List<String>>[];
672:     for (final row in rows) {
673:       final rank = <String>[];
674:       for (var i = 0; i < row.length; i++) {
675:         final char = row[i];
676:         if (RegExp(r'[1-8]').hasMatch(char)) {
677:           final count = int.parse(char);
678:           for (var j = 0; j < count; j++) {
679:             rank.add('');
680:           }
681:         } else {
682:           switch (char) {
683:             case 'P': rank.add('♙'); break;
684:             case 'N': rank.add('♘'); break;
685:             case 'B': rank.add('♗'); break;
686:             case 'R': rank.add('♖'); break;
687:             case 'Q': rank.add('♕'); break;
688:             case 'K': rank.add('♔'); break;
689:             case 'p': rank.add('♟'); break;
690:             case 'n': rank.add('♞'); break;
691:             case 'b': rank.add('♝'); break;
692:             case 'r': rank.add('♜'); break;
693:             case 'q': rank.add('♛'); break;
694:             case 'k': rank.add('♚'); break;
695:             default: rank.add(''); break;
696:           }
697:         }
698:       }
699:       grid.add(rank);
700:     }
701:     return grid;
702:   }
703: }

# T15.1D — Add piece drag-and-drop interaction alongside tap-to-move

This is an interaction-layer improvement.

The existing tap-to-move interaction MUST remain fully functional.

Do NOT redesign the board.

Do NOT rewrite the review architecture.

Do NOT introduce unrelated features.

==================================================
MOST IMPORTANT CONSTRAINT
=========================

The current move pipeline is already correct:

board interaction
→ `_onSquareTapped`
→ `_submitMove`
→ `ReviewService.processAnswer`
→ review result / SRS progression

Do not bypass this pipeline.

Drag-and-drop must ultimately produce the SAME normalized from/to move that tap-to-move currently produces.

Conceptually:

drag source → drag destination
↓
existing move path
↓
_submitMove(from,to)

Do not create a second review/move-validation system.

==================================================
STEP 1 — INSPECT CURRENT IMPLEMENTATION
=======================================

Before editing anything:

Inspect:

* `_buildBoard()`
* square widgets
* piece widgets/images
* `_selectedSquare`
* `_onSquareTapped()`
* `_submitMove()`
* existing coordinate conversion
* current board gesture handling

Determine the smallest way to add drag support without replacing the current interaction path.

Do not immediately create a new "BoardController."

Only introduce a controller/helper if the current code genuinely requires one.

==================================================
SUPPORTED INTERACTIONS
======================

The board must support BOTH:

1. Tap-to-move
2. Drag-and-drop

Tap-to-move must continue working exactly as before.

Examples:

Tap e2
→ selected e2

Tap e4
→ existing move submission

Drag e2 → e4
→ same move submission

==================================================
DRAG SOURCE
===========

Dragging should begin from an occupied piece square.

Do not allow empty squares to behave like draggable pieces.

Do not alter selection semantics unnecessarily.

The dragged piece should visually follow the pointer/finger.

==================================================
DRAG VISUAL
===========

While dragging:

* piece should remain clearly visible
* piece should follow the pointer naturally
* dragged piece should not appear incorrectly duplicated
* underlying source square may temporarily suppress the normal piece rendering
* board geometry must remain unchanged

Keep the drag visual simple.

Do NOT implement fancy animation in this task.

==================================================
DROP HANDLING
=============

When the pointer/finger is released:

1. convert the drop position into a board square
2. validate that the destination is inside the board
3. submit the resulting from/to pair through the existing move path

Do not perform new chess legality checks inside the UI.

Do not decide repertoire correctness inside the UI.

Do not update SRS inside the UI.

The existing services remain authoritative.

==================================================
INVALID DROPS
=============

For now, invalid drops should safely cancel.

Examples:

* outside board
* source/destination cannot be resolved

Do not introduce dialogs.

Do not introduce new error UI.

Do not modify review semantics.

==================================================
ORIENTATION
===========

The drag coordinate mapping must respect the board's actual orientation.

Verify:

* White-oriented board
* Black-oriented board if the current board supports it

The visual source/destination square must correspond to the correct algebraic square.

Do not duplicate orientation algorithms if one already exists.

==================================================
PROMOTION
=========

If the existing application already has promotion support, preserve it.

If promotion is currently unsupported by the existing review interaction, do NOT redesign promotion as part of this task.

Do not invent a full promotion UI just because dragging exposes the possibility.

Document the limitation if relevant.

==================================================
GESTURE CONFLICTS
=================

Be careful not to break:

* ordinary taps
* board scrolling outside the board
* mouse interaction
* touch interaction

Do not make the entire application non-scrollable merely to support dragging.

The board should consume pointer gestures only where appropriate.

==================================================
ARCHITECTURE
============

Keep rendering and interaction separate conceptually:

Board rendering:

* squares
* pieces
* highlights

Interaction:

* pointer down
* drag position
* pointer up
* source/destination conversion

Review logic remains outside both.

Do not put ReviewService calls into low-level piece widgets.

==================================================
IMPLEMENTATION SIZE
===================

Prefer the smallest viable implementation.

It is acceptable to:

* add a small drag state object
* add a small helper for board coordinate conversion
* add gesture handling around the existing board

It is NOT acceptable to:

* rewrite the entire board
* replace the existing board with another framework
* introduce a state-management package
* introduce a large controller architecture
* move logic into new layers without necessity

==================================================
TESTING
=======

Add a focused test suite for drag interaction where practical.

At minimum, test the logical interaction path:

source square
→ destination square
→ existing move callback

Test:

* valid drag
* invalid/outside drop
* tap interaction still works
* orientation-aware mapping if supported by existing architecture

Do not write brittle gesture tests that depend unnecessarily on exact screen pixels.

Preserve all existing tests.

==================================================
VERIFICATION
============

Implement in the smallest possible increment.

After implementation:

1. run targeted tests
2. run:

   ./verify

If verification fails:

STOP.

Do not continue improving the drag behavior until the failure is fixed.

==================================================
REGRESSION CHECK
================

Explicitly confirm:

* tap-to-move still works
* drag-to-move works
* both use the same `_submitMove()` path
* `_onSquareTapped()` behavior remains intact
* ReviewService is unchanged
* ReviewEngine is unchanged
* SRS behavior is unchanged
* responsive sizing is unchanged
* selection highlights still work
* last-move highlights still work
* SVG pieces still render correctly

==================================================
DO NOT IMPLEMENT
================

Do not implement:

* piece animation
* snap animation
* legal-move dots
* premoves
* arrows
* board sounds
* haptic feedback
* promotion redesign
* move clocks
* engine analysis
* board themes
* settings
* study tree
* navigation redesign

Those are separate bricks.

==================================================
STOP CONDITION
==============

When drag-and-drop works and:

```
./verify
```

passes cleanly:

STOP.

Do not implement T15.1E.

Do not perform unrelated cleanup.

Before adding ANY new feature such as sound or haptics, perform one small stabilization brick:

# T15.1D.1 — Validate and polish the existing drag interaction

This is NOT a feature expansion.

The goal is to ensure the current T15.1D implementation is actually a good chess interaction and does not contain obvious UX/interaction defects.

==================================================
IMPORTANT
=========

Do NOT add:

* sound
* haptics
* animation
* legal-move dots
* arrows
* promotion UI
* premoves
* board themes
* settings
* navigation
* review redesign

Do NOT add another interaction system.

Do NOT replace Draggable/DragTarget unless inspection proves the current implementation is fundamentally broken.

==================================================
FIRST — INSPECT
===============

Read the current T15.1D implementation carefully.

Inspect:

* Draggable configuration
* DragTarget configuration
* drag feedback widget
* source-square rendering
* destination handling
* selection handling
* gesture interaction
* board coordinate mapping
* orientation mapping

Identify any concrete problems.

Do not make speculative refactors.

==================================================
CHECK THESE SPECIFIC CASES
==========================

Verify:

1. Drag a piece from a normal square to another square.
2. Drag a piece onto an occupied square.
3. Drag a piece to an invalid location.
4. Start dragging and cancel/release outside the board.
5. Tap a piece.
6. Tap a destination.
7. Tap a different piece after selecting one.
8. Drag after a previous tap-selection.
9. Tap after a previous drag.
10. Drag on a narrow viewport.
11. Drag on a wide viewport.

Also verify the conceptual behavior under both board orientations if orientation support exists.

==================================================
DRAG FEEDBACK
=============

Inspect the drag preview.

The dragged piece should:

* look like the actual piece
* remain appropriately sized
* follow the pointer/finger naturally
* not appear as an enormous copy
* not leave an unwanted duplicate on the source square
* not distort
* not affect board geometry

Do not create elaborate animations.

A clean static drag preview is sufficient.

==================================================
SELECTION STATE
===============

Check for state conflicts between:

* `_selectedSquare`
* drag source
* drag destination
* last-move highlights

Dragging must not leave stale selection state.

A canceled drag must not corrupt selection state.

A completed drag must not create a second independent move-processing path.

==================================================
MOVE SUBMISSION
===============

This invariant MUST remain true:

tap interaction → existing `_submitMove()`

drag interaction → existing `_submitMove()`

No duplicate validation.

No direct ReviewService invocation from drag widgets.

No direct SRS manipulation.

==================================================
SMALL FIXES ONLY
================

If you discover problems, fix only those problems.

Do not turn this into a board rewrite.

Examples of acceptable fixes:

* incorrect drag preview size
* source piece remaining visible incorrectly during drag
* stale selection after drop
* incorrect target-square mapping
* invalid drops not being safely canceled
* drag/tap conflict

Examples of unacceptable scope:

* creating a new board controller
* moving all board code into a new architecture
* rewriting ReviewPage
* introducing a state-management framework
* redesigning the entire gesture system

==================================================
TESTS
=====

Add focused tests only for actual defects/behavior found.

Do not create a huge gesture-test framework.

Do not modify unrelated tests.

==================================================
VERIFICATION
============

After the smallest fixes:

1. run targeted tests
2. run:

   ./verify

If ./verify fails:

STOP and fix it.

Do not continue to another task.

==================================================
STOP CONDITION
==============

Once the current drag implementation is demonstrably stable and ./verify passes:

STOP.

Do not implement sound/haptics.

Report:

STEP COMPLETED:
T15.1D.1 — Drag interaction validation/polish

PROBLEMS FOUND:
<none, or exact problems>

VERIFY: <exact result>

T15.1E — Sound/tactile feedback cues

Then stop.

643:           }

# T15.1E — Add restrained sound/tactile feedback cues

==================================================
CORE PRINCIPLE
==============

Feedback is PRESENTATION ONLY.

It must never become part of:

* move validation
* SRS logic
* ReviewService
* ReviewEngine
* persistence
* board state
* move correctness

The existing review flow must remain authoritative.

A sound/haptic failure must NEVER prevent a move from being processed.

==================================================
BEFORE EDITING
==============

Inspect the current implementation of:

* `_submitMove()`
* review result handling
* existing Flutter dependencies
* pubspec.yaml
* available assets

Determine the safest point at which feedback can be triggered AFTER the existing move result is known.

Do not modify ReviewService or ReviewEngine just to add feedback.

==================================================
FEEDBACK EVENTS
===============

Support only these initial events:

1. successful repertoire move
2. incorrect repertoire move

Optionally distinguish a capture only if it can be determined without modifying business logic.

Do NOT add a large audio taxonomy.

Do NOT add:

* move sounds for every intermediate state
* navigation sounds
* UI click sounds
* celebratory music
* streak sounds
* XP/gamification sounds

==================================================
SOUND
=====

Use the smallest appropriate implementation.

Before adding a package:

1. inspect pubspec.yaml
2. determine whether an audio package already exists
3. prefer Flutter/native capabilities if sufficient
4. otherwise add only the minimum necessary dependency

Do not add multiple audio packages.

Use very short, restrained sounds.

The aesthetic should fit PRODUCT.md:

quiet
immediate
non-gamified
non-intrusive

The sound must not dominate the chessboard experience.

Do not download random external sound assets.

Use assets already in the repository where appropriate, or create/use a minimal legally safe local asset strategy.

==================================================
HAPTICS
=======

Add very light tactile feedback only if it can be implemented safely for supported platforms.

Important:

* desktop/web may not support meaningful haptics
* unsupported platforms must gracefully do nothing
* no runtime errors
* no platform-specific complexity that is disproportionate to this feature

Do not add haptic behavior that blocks or delays move submission.

==================================================
USER CONTROL
============

Do NOT build a complete settings screen.

For this brick, it is acceptable to implement the feedback in a simple way.

If the project already has a preference mechanism, use it.

Otherwise, do not introduce a large settings architecture just to add a toggle.

If a toggle is needed to avoid always-on behavior, use the smallest appropriate existing mechanism and document it.

==================================================
TIMING
======

Feedback should occur after the application already knows whether the submitted move was correct.

Conceptually:

_user submits move
↓
existing ReviewService / ReviewEngine
↓
existing result
↓
update existing UI
↓
optional sound/haptic side effect

Never:

_sound/haptic
↓
move processing
↓
review result

==================================================
FAILURE ISOLATION
=================

Audio/haptic errors must be isolated.

If:

* an asset is missing
* playback fails
* a platform does not support haptics
* an audio API throws

the chess review interaction must continue normally.

Never propagate feedback failures into move validation.

==================================================
TESTING
=======

Do not attempt complex audio integration tests unless the repository already has infrastructure for them.

At minimum, make sure:

* normal move processing remains testable
* feedback calls do not alter review results
* unsupported feedback paths fail safely

Keep tests deterministic.

Do not rewrite existing tests.

==================================================
SCOPE LIMITS
============

Do NOT implement:

* settings UI
* volume mixer
* sound themes
* multiple piece sound sets
* animations
* move arrows
* legal move indicators
* promotion UI
* board themes
* navigation redesign
* study tree
* review redesign
* new state-management architecture

==================================================
VERIFICATION
============

Work in the smallest possible increment.

After implementation:

1. run targeted tests
2. run:

   ./verify

If ./verify fails:

STOP.

Fix the failure before doing anything else.

==================================================
REGRESSION CHECK
================

Confirm:

* tap moves still work
* drag moves still work
* incorrect moves still become incorrect
* correct moves still become correct
* SRS updates are unchanged
* auto-continuation is unchanged
* persistence is unchanged
* board rendering is unchanged
* feedback cannot block review processing

==================================================
IMPORTANT STOP RULE
===================

Do not automatically add additional polish after sound/haptics works.

When the feature is implemented and ./verify passes:

STOP.

Report:

STEP COMPLETED:
T15.1E — Sound/tactile feedback

FILES CHANGED: <exact list>

DEPENDENCIES:
<exact changes, ideally none>

FEEDBACK EVENTS: <exact events implemented>

FAILURE ISOLATION: <how feedback failures are prevented from affecting review>

TESTS: <exact result>

VERIFY: <exact result>

NEXT SAFE BRICK:
T15.1F — <one small next step>

Then stop.

### 🤖 Assistant
*2026-09-14 00:33:26*

*[step-start part]*

CURRENT TREE MODEL:
- `Study` contains a list of chapters.
- `Chapter` contains a `PositionNode? root` and metadata (title, starting FEN).
- `PositionNode` represents a node in the move tree, containing its own unique ID, FEN, incoming `RepertoireMove?`, list of comments/NAGs, and list of `children` (supporting recursive annotation variations).
- Repository APIs (`StudyRepository`, `InMemoryStudyRepository`, `LocalStudyRepository`) provide methods to fetch all studies, get chapters by ID, save studies/chapters/position trees, and retrieve decisions.

CURRENT NAVIGATION:
- `ReviewPage` currently operates as a review-first queue. It fetches due decisions via `ReviewService.getDueDecisions(studyId: _scopedStudyId)` and displays the chessboard for the current active `PositionNode`.
- The user can scope reviews by all studies or a specific study using a Material `Drawer` that displays due counts and study titles, but there is currently no visual notation tree sidebar to explore the moves, chapters, variations, or jump to arbitrary positions in a study.

REUSE OPPORTUNITIES:
- Existing `Study`, `Chapter`, and `PositionNode` models and repository methods are fully mature, persisted, and tested.
- `_findNodeById` and FEN-to-board rendering logic in `ReviewPage` can be utilized to inspect or preview nodes.
- Existing Material widgets (`ExpansionTile`, `ListView`, `ListTile`) can render a lightweight, collapsible move/chapter notation tree.

T16.1 SMALLEST BRICK:
- Add a collapsible study/chapter notation sidebar (or expandable side panel in `ReviewPage`) displaying chapters and root moves for the active study, allowing the user to inspect the repertoire tree without altering ReviewService core execution paths.

EXPECTED FILES:
- `lib/main.dart` (or a dedicated small widget file under `lib/ui/`)

DO NOT TOUCH:
- `lib/domain/**`
- `lib/application/**`
- `lib/persistence/**`
- `lib/chess/**`
- Test files

RISKS:
- Overcomplicating the sidebar into a full two-way interactive board editor or duplicating SRS/review state in the view layer.
- Breaking responsive desktop/mobile layouts if the sidebar doesn't collapse or scale cleanly.

TEST PLAN:

This is an EXPLORATION / PRESENTATION feature.

The tree must NOT modify SRS state.

==================================================

===================

T16.1 is READ-ONLY.

* jump to an arbitrary position
* edit moves
* create variations
* delete moves
* modify repertoire data
* change SRS state
* alter the review queue

Node selection/navigation belongs to T16.2.

7: /// A node in the repertoire tree representing a chess position and its move
8: /// relationships. Each child edge is a move from this position.
9: class PositionNode extends Equatable {
10:   const PositionNode({
11:     required this.id,
12:     required this.positionKey,
13:     this.fen = '',
14:     this.children = const [],
15:     this.incomingMove,
16:     this.comment,
17:   });
18: 
19:   factory PositionNode.create({
20:     required PositionKey positionKey,
21:     String? fen,
22:     PositionNode? parent,
23:     RepertoireMove? incomingMove,
24:     String? comment,
25:   }) {
26:     final resolvedFen = fen ?? positionKey.fen;
27:     return PositionNode(
28:       id: uuid.v4(),
29:       positionKey: positionKey,
30:       fen: resolvedFen,
31:       incomingMove: incomingMove,
32:       comment: comment,
33:       children: const [],
34:     );
35:   }
36: 
37:   final String id;
38: 
39:   final PositionKey positionKey;
40: 
41:   final String fen;
42: 
43:   final RepertoireMove? incomingMove;
44: 
45:   final String? comment;
46: 
47:   final List<PositionNode> children;
48: 
49:   bool get isLeaf => children.isEmpty;
50: 
51:   PositionNode addChild(PositionNode child) {
52:     return PositionNode(
53:       id: id,
54:       positionKey: positionKey,
55:       fen: fen,
56:       incomingMove: incomingMove,
57:       comment: comment,
58:       children: [...children, child],
59:     );
60:   }
61: 
62:   PositionNode? childForMove(RepertoireMove move) {
63:     for (final child in children) {
64:       if (child.incomingMove?.from == move.from && child.incomingMove?.to == move.to && child.incomingMove?.promotion == move.promotion) {
65:         return child;
66:       }
67:     }
68:     return null;
69:   }
70: 
71:   List<RepertoireMove> get childMoves => children.map((c) => c.incomingMove!).toList();
72: 
73:   @override
74:   bool get stringify => true;
75: 
76:   @override
77:   List<Object?> get props => [id, positionKey, incomingMove, children, comment];
78: }

Now implement EXACTLY ONE brick:

# T16.2 — Select a tree node to preview that position

==================================================
CORE DESIGN DECISION
====================

Tree selection is EXPLORATION, not SRS review.

Clicking a tree node must NOT silently replace the current review question or mutate review/SRS state.

Instead:

tree node selected
↓
preview that PositionNode
↓
board displays its position
↓
user can inspect it
↓
explicit action returns to active review

This keeps study exploration separate from the SRS review state machine.

==================================================
STEP 1 — INSPECT
================

Before editing:

Inspect:

* current `StudyTreeSidebar`
* ReviewPage state
* `_currentNode`
* current review-queue state
* how FEN is converted to the board
* how the current drawer is opened/closed
* whether there is already a notion of "current review" versus "current board position"

Determine the smallest way to introduce a preview state.

Do NOT modify ReviewService or ReviewEngine.

==================================================
PREVIEW STATE
=============

Introduce the smallest presentation-level state necessary.

Conceptually:

activeReviewNode
previewNode (nullable)
isPreviewing

But do NOT use these exact names if the existing structure suggests something simpler.

The key invariant:

PREVIEWING MUST NOT ALTER THE SRS REVIEW ITEM.

Do not:

* mark items reviewed
* change due dates
* change streak
* update lapses
* alter review queues

==================================================
TREE CLICK
==========

When the user taps/selects a PositionNode:

1. identify the PositionNode
2. close the tree drawer if appropriate
3. display that node's FEN on the board
4. clearly indicate that the board is in preview mode
5. do NOT send anything to ReviewService

The preview should work for:

* root/main-line positions
* nested variations
* positions from different chapters

==================================================
PREVIEW UX
==========

The user must be able to tell that the board is showing an explored study position rather than the active SRS question.

Use restrained UI.

Possible indication:

* small "Preview" label
* subtle toolbar state
* highlighted tree selection

Do NOT build a large banner.

==================================================
RETURN TO REVIEW
================

Provide one explicit, obvious way to return to the active review position.

For example:

"Back to review"

or an equally compact control appropriate to the current UI.

When returning:

* restore the active review node
* restore normal review mode
* restore normal move submission behavior

Do NOT reset or modify SRS state merely by entering/leaving preview.

==================================================
MOVE INPUT IN PREVIEW
=====================

For this milestone, preview should be READ-ONLY.

Do not allow preview moves to be submitted to ReviewService.

Do not create a second analysis/game mode.

The board may display the position, but normal review move submission must be disabled while previewing.

Make the visual state obvious enough that the user understands this.

==================================================
BOARD
=====

Do NOT modify board rendering architecture.

Reuse the existing:

* SVG pieces
* responsive geometry
* selection highlights
* last-move highlights
* orientation handling
* board rendering

Only provide it with the preview PositionNode/FEN.

==================================================
TREE STATE
==========

If practical, keep the selected tree node highlighted while previewing.

Do not build persistent tree-selection infrastructure.

Do not add URL routing or deep links.

==================================================
ARCHITECTURE
============

Keep the separation:

StudyTreeSidebar
↓
selection callback
↓
ReviewPage presentation state
↓
board rendering

The sidebar must NOT:

* call ReviewService
* call ReviewEngine
* update SRS
* modify repository data

==================================================
TESTING
=======

Add focused tests for:

1. selecting a tree node enters preview
2. selected node's FEN is displayed
3. preview does not call review processing
4. preview move submission is disabled
5. returning to review restores the prior active review node

Keep the tests deterministic.

Do not modify existing business-logic tests.

==================================================
IMPLEMENTATION PROCESS
======================

Do this as small internal steps:

STEP A
Add preview state without changing tree behavior.

Verify.

STEP B
Wire tree selection to preview state.

Verify.

STEP C
Display preview position on board.

Verify.

STEP D
Add return-to-review control.

Verify.

Do NOT combine all four steps into one giant edit.

==================================================
DEPENDENCIES
============

Do not add a dependency.

Use the existing Flutter implementation unless inspection proves otherwise.

==================================================
STRICTLY OUT OF SCOPE
=====================

Do NOT implement:

* editing positions
* editing moves
* creating variations
* deleting nodes
* playing moves in preview
* analysis mode
* engine evaluation
* autoplay
* move list playback
* bookmarks
* annotations editor
* study management
* navigation redesign
* new state-management framework

==================================================
VERIFICATION
============

After every internal step:

1. run targeted tests
2. run:

   ./verify

If verification fails:

STOP.

Fix the failure before continuing.

Never weaken or delete tests to get a green result.

==================================================
REGRESSION CHECK
================

Confirm:

* normal review still starts normally
* active review node remains intact
* tap-to-move still works in review mode
* drag-to-move still works in review mode
* correct/incorrect processing is unchanged
* SRS is unchanged
* haptics are unchanged
* board rendering is unchanged
* tree remains readable
* preview cannot accidentally submit a review answer

==================================================
STOP CONDITION
==============

When T16.2 works and ./verify passes:

STOP.

Do not implement T16.3.

Report:

STEP COMPLETED:
T16.2 — Tree-node position preview

FILES CHANGED: <exact list>

PREVIEW MODEL: <how preview state is represented>

REVIEW ISOLATION:
<how preview avoids SRS/review processing>

RETURN TO REVIEW: <how the user returns>

TESTS: <exact result>

VERIFY: <exact result>

REGRESSION CHECK: <brief confirmation>

NEXT SAFE BRICK:
T16.3 — <one small next step>

Then stop.
