PREVIEW MODEL:
Introduced `_previewNode` in `_ReviewPageState`. When a node in the Study Tree sidebar is tapped, it sets `_previewNode` to that node, closes the drawer, and renders its FEN on the board while displaying a restrained read-only preview banner and highlight.

REVIEW ISOLATION:
Preview is strictly read-only. Interactive move taps and moves are blocked while `_previewNode` is active (`_onSquareTapped` early-returns), ensuring zero interaction with `ReviewService`, `ReviewEngine`, or SRS review records.

RETURN TO REVIEW:
Provided an explicit "Return to Review" button in the preview banner that clears `_previewNode` and immediately restores the active review item and normal review interaction.

# T16.3 — Show the move path/context for the previewed position

==================================================
OBJECTIVE
=========

When the user previews a PositionNode from the study tree, make it possible to understand the move sequence that led to that position.

The goal is to make the study tree feel more like a real chess study browser, inspired by Listudy.

This is still READ-ONLY exploration.

==================================================
CORE RULE
=========

Do NOT change:

* ReviewService
* ReviewEngine
* SRS state
* review queue
* persistence
* PGN data
* PositionNode domain model

Do NOT introduce a second chess-tree model.

The existing Study / Chapter / PositionNode structure remains authoritative.

==================================================
FIRST — INSPECT
===============

Before editing, inspect:

* PositionNode structure
* parent/child relationships
* incoming RepertoireMove
* chapter root
* current `_previewNode`
* StudyTreeSidebar
* existing notation available in the domain

Determine the simplest way to reconstruct the path from the chapter root to `_previewNode`.

Do NOT assume parent pointers exist.

If PositionNode has no parent reference, derive the path by traversing the existing tree.

Do not modify the domain model merely to add parent pointers.

==================================================
PATH DISPLAY
============

For the currently previewed node, display a compact move path such as:

1. e4 e5
2. Nf3 Nc6
3. Bb5 a6

or an equivalent notation appropriate to the current data.

The path should include the moves from the chapter starting position through the selected node.

The current node should be visually identifiable.

==================================================
VARIATIONS
==========

The path must respect the actual variation branch.

Example:

main line:

1. e4 e5 2. Nf3

variation:

1. e4 c5 2. Nf3

If the user previews the `c5` branch, the displayed path must be:

1. e4 c5 ...

Do not accidentally flatten all branches into the main line.

==================================================
UI PLACEMENT
============

Keep this compact.

Suitable locations:

* preview banner
* small move strip beneath/above board
* compact panel adjacent to board

Use the existing ReviewPage layout.

Do NOT create a large analysis panel.

The chessboard remains dominant.

==================================================
SCROLLING
=========

If the path is long:

* keep it horizontally/vertically scrollable as appropriate
* keep the current move visible when practical
* do not let a long move sequence break the overall layout

Do not build a sophisticated move-list editor.

==================================================
CURRENT REVIEW MODE
===================

When NOT previewing:

Do not add a large permanent move-list UI.

This feature is specifically for the preview experience.

Normal review should remain minimal.

==================================================
TREE SYNCHRONIZATION
====================

When previewing a node:

* the corresponding tree node remains highlighted
* the path corresponds to the same PositionNode
* the board corresponds to the same PositionNode

These three visual representations must agree.

Do not create duplicate state if the existing `_previewNode` is sufficient.

==================================================
INTERACTION
===========

The move path is READ-ONLY.

Do not allow:

* clicking moves to alter preview position
* editing SAN
* deleting moves
* creating variations
* autoplay
* engine analysis

Navigation through the tree remains the existing T16.2 mechanism.

==================================================
IMPLEMENTATION PROCESS
======================

Work incrementally:

STEP A
Implement path reconstruction from chapter root to preview node.

Run tests + ./verify.

STEP B
Render the path in the preview UI.

Run tests + ./verify.

STEP C
Add current-node indication within the path.

Run tests + ./verify.

Do not combine all steps into one giant change.

==================================================
TESTING
=======

Add focused tests for:

1. root node path
2. linear main-line path
3. nested variation path
4. selected preview node appears as current
5. path does not mutate review state
6. returning to review removes preview-only path UI

Do not alter existing business logic tests.

Do not create brittle pixel tests.

==================================================
DEPENDENCIES
============

Do not add any dependency.

Use existing Dart/Flutter functionality.

==================================================
STRICTLY OUT OF SCOPE
=====================

Do NOT implement:

* T17 statistics
* review history charts
* dashboards
* analytics
* session summaries
* study editing
* move editing
* study management
* engine analysis
* animations
* sound changes
* settings
* cloud features

==================================================
VERIFICATION
============

After every implementation step:

1. run targeted tests
2. run:

   ./verify

If ./verify fails:

STOP.

Fix the failure first.

Never weaken tests to obtain a green result.

==================================================
REGRESSION CHECK
================

Confirm:

* review state unchanged
* SRS unchanged
* normal review UI unchanged outside preview
* board rendering unchanged
* tree behavior unchanged
* drag/tap review behavior unchanged
* preview remains read-only
* Return to Review still works
* responsive board geometry unchanged

==================================================
STOP CONDITION
==============

Once T16.3 works and ./verify passes:

STOP.

Do NOT implement T17.1.

Report:

STEP COMPLETED:
T16.3 — Preview move path/context

FILES CHANGED: <exact list>

PATH RECONSTRUCTION: <brief explanation>

VARIATION HANDLING: <brief explanation>

UI: <where the path is displayed>

TESTS: <exact result>

VERIFY: <exact result>

REGRESSION CHECK: <brief confirmation>

T16.4 — <one small next step>

Then stop.

Now implement EXACTLY ONE brick:

# T16.4 — Navigate through study positions from the tree/preview

==================================================
OBJECTIVE
=========

Make the Listudy-inspired study browser actually navigable.

The user should be able to move from the currently previewed position to nearby positions in the SAME study/variation without affecting the SRS review queue.

This remains EXPLORATION.

==================================================
CRITICAL STATE SEPARATION
=========================

There are still two different concepts:

ACTIVE REVIEW

* controlled by the existing review queue
* SRS state
* ReviewService / ReviewEngine
* answer processing

STUDY PREVIEW

* user browsing repertoire
* read-only
* no SRS updates
* no review answer processing

Do NOT merge these states.

Do NOT make arbitrary study navigation overwrite the active review item.

==================================================
FIRST — INSPECT
===============

Inspect the actual current implementation of:

* `_previewNode`
* `_currentNode`
* `StudyTreeSidebar`
* `_findPathToNode`
* current preview move strip
* chapter/PositionNode hierarchy

Determine the smallest useful navigation interaction.

==================================================
INITIAL NAVIGATION SCOPE
========================

Implement only adjacent navigation:

* previous position
* next position

within the currently previewed branch.

Navigation should follow the actual PositionNode tree.

For example:

e4 → e5 → Nf3 → Nc6

allows:

Nf3
← e5
→ Nc6

Do NOT jump between unrelated branches automatically.

==================================================
VARIATIONS
==========

Handle variation branches deliberately.

If a node has multiple children, do NOT silently choose an arbitrary child.

Instead, use the existing tree UI to select another branch.

The navigation control should only move through the currently selected path where that path is unambiguous.

If necessary, disable "Next" when multiple unexplored branches exist rather than guessing.

==================================================
TREE SYNCHRONIZATION
====================

When preview navigation changes the node:

* board changes
* current tree node highlight changes
* move path changes

All three must refer to the same PositionNode.

Do not introduce duplicate PositionNode state.

Use `_previewNode` as the presentation source of truth unless the existing implementation requires otherwise.

==================================================
UI
==

Add minimal controls to the preview UI.

For example:

[Previous]   move/path   [Next]

or equivalent compact controls.

The controls should:

* be obvious
* be small
* not dominate the board
* remain consistent with the restrained design
* work on desktop and mobile

Do NOT create a new navigation bar.

==================================================
KEYBOARD SUPPORT
================

If adding keyboard shortcuts can be done very simply using existing Flutter APIs, it is acceptable to support:

Left Arrow = previous
Right Arrow = next

But this is OPTIONAL.

Do NOT introduce a keyboard-command framework.

==================================================
READ-ONLY
=========

Navigation must remain read-only.

Do not allow the preview board to submit moves.

Do not call:

* ReviewService
* ReviewEngine
* Scheduler
* persistence mutation APIs

==================================================
TREE DATA
=========

Do not alter:

* Study
* Chapter
* PositionNode
* repository APIs
* PGN representation

Do not add parent pointers.

Do not create a duplicate navigation model.

If previous-node lookup requires traversing from the chapter root, reuse the existing tree traversal approach.

==================================================
IMPLEMENTATION PROCESS
======================

Work in small steps.

STEP A
Implement previous-node lookup for the active preview path.

Run tests + ./verify.

STEP B
Implement next-node lookup where unambiguous.

Run tests + ./verify.

STEP C
Add compact Previous/Next controls.

Run tests + ./verify.

STEP D
Synchronize board/tree/path state.

Run tests + ./verify.

Do NOT combine all steps into one large rewrite.

==================================================
TESTING
=======

Add focused tests for:

1. previous navigation on a linear branch
2. next navigation on a linear branch
3. root node has no previous
4. leaf node has no next
5. branch ambiguity does not silently select an arbitrary branch
6. navigation updates preview node
7. navigation does not modify active review/SRS state

Do not weaken existing tests.

==================================================
DEPENDENCIES
============

No new dependencies.

==================================================
STRICTLY OUT OF SCOPE
=====================

Do NOT implement:

* editing moves
* creating/deleting variations
* autoplay
* engine analysis
* statistics
* charts
* review-session analytics
* study management
* comments editor
* annotations editor
* cloud sync
* new navigation architecture

==================================================
VERIFICATION
============

After every implementation step:

1. targeted tests
2. ./verify

If ./verify fails:

STOP.

Fix the problem before proceeding.

==================================================
REGRESSION CHECK
================

Confirm:

* normal review still works
* active review node is untouched by preview navigation
* SRS state untouched
* review submission untouched
* tap-to-move untouched
* drag-to-move untouched
* haptics untouched
* board geometry untouched
* tree rendering remains intact
* preview remains read-only
* Return to Review restores normal review correctly

==================================================
STOP CONDITION
==============

When T16.4 is complete and ./verify passes:

STOP.

Do not implement T17.1.

Report:

STEP COMPLETED:
T16.4 — Study preview navigation

NAVIGATION MODEL: <brief explanation>

VARIATION BEHAVIOR: <brief explanation>

REVIEW ISOLATION: <brief confirmation>

VERIFY: <exact result>

Then stop.

Now implement EXACTLY ONE brick:

# T17.1 — Minimal review status summary

==================================================
IMPORTANT PRODUCT CONSTRAINT
============================

This is NOT a dashboard.

PRODUCT.md explicitly defines the product as review-first and rejects dashboard/social/gamification clutter.

Therefore:

DO NOT build:

* charts
* graphs
* interval histograms
* XP
* streak counters
* achievement systems
* study analytics dashboards
* large statistics cards
* progress rings
* session-history screens

The objective is only to give the user a small amount of useful orientation while reviewing.

==================================================
OBJECTIVE
=========

Add a very compact review-status indicator to the normal Review UI.

It should answer simple questions such as:

* Are there items left to review?
* How much review remains?
* Is the review queue currently empty?

Keep the information minimal.

Example concept:

12 due

or:

12 remaining

or:

Up to date

The exact wording should follow the existing application's terminology.

==================================================
DATA SOURCE
===========

Use the EXISTING review/application data.

Inspect what ReviewService already exposes.

Do NOT create a second statistics engine.

Do NOT recalculate SRS state independently inside the UI.

Do NOT inspect persistence directly from the UI to calculate statistics.

The application/domain layers remain the source of truth.

==================================================
SCOPE
=====

This is a presentation-only feature.

The summary may update when:

* the review queue changes
* a review answer is processed
* the active study scope changes

But it must not change the review logic itself.

==================================================
PLACEMENT
=========

Keep it compact.

Good locations include:

* a small area in the review header
* a subtle line near the review controls
* a compact status label above/below the board

Do NOT place it over the chessboard.

Do NOT reduce the board unnecessarily.

The board remains the dominant visual element.

==================================================
EMPTY QUEUE
===========

When there are no due items, show the existing calm idle state with a small clear indication that review is currently up to date.

Do not introduce a modal.

Do not celebrate completion.

No confetti.

No sound.

No new gamification.

==================================================
STUDY SCOPE
===========

If the user is currently filtering to a study, the displayed count should correspond to the active review scope.

If viewing all studies, it should correspond to all active due items.

Do not create a separate counting algorithm if ReviewService already provides the relevant information.

==================================================
PREVIEW MODE
============

When `_previewNode` is active:

Do not present the status as though the preview position were part of the active review queue.

The preview experience should remain clearly separate from normal review.

The existing preview UI should remain intact.

==================================================
RESPONSIVENESS
==============

The summary must work on:

* mobile
* tablet
* desktop

It should remain unobtrusive on narrow screens.

Do not redesign the responsive layout.

==================================================
IMPLEMENTATION PROCESS
======================

DO NOT implement the entire thing in one giant change.

STEP A
Inspect existing ReviewService/application APIs for due-count information.

Do not modify them.

If no suitable API exists, determine the smallest safe presentation-facing way to obtain the already-known count.

Verify.

STEP B
Render the compact summary in normal Review mode.

Verify.

STEP C
Update the summary after review actions / scope changes.

Verify.

STEP D
Handle the empty-review state if necessary.

Verify.

==================================================
TESTING
=======

Add focused tests for:

1. due-count displayed correctly
2. count changes after a review answer when the existing queue changes
3. study filtering displays the scoped count
4. empty queue displays the calm idle state
5. preview mode does not incorrectly present preview as an active review item

Do not build an elaborate statistics test suite.

Do not modify business-logic tests merely to accommodate the UI.

==================================================
DEPENDENCIES
============

No new dependencies.

==================================================
DO NOT TOUCH
============

Do not modify:

* domain SRS models
* scheduler
* ReviewEngine
* persistence behavior
* chess logic
* board rendering
* drag interaction
* study-tree implementation

unless an absolutely minimal integration change is unavoidable.

==================================================
REGRESSION CHECK
================

Confirm:

* review queue behavior unchanged
* SRS scheduling unchanged
* move validation unchanged
* tap-to-move unchanged
* drag-to-move unchanged
* haptics unchanged
* board geometry unchanged
* study tree unchanged
* preview mode unchanged
* Return to Review unchanged

==================================================
VERIFICATION
============

After every implementation step:

1. targeted tests
2. ./verify

If ./verify fails:

STOP.

Fix it before proceeding.

Never delete/weaken tests to make verification pass.

==================================================
STOP CONDITION
==============

When T17.1 is complete and ./verify passes:

STOP.

Do NOT implement interval charts or additional statistics.

Final report:

STEP COMPLETED:
T17.1 — Minimal review status summary

FILES CHANGED: <exact list>

DATA SOURCE:
<existing API/state used>

DISPLAY: <exact summary shown>

EMPTY STATE: <exact behavior>

TESTS: <exact result>

VERIFY: <exact result>

REGRESSION CHECK: <brief confirmation>

NEXT SAFE BRICK: <one small next step>

Then stop.

# T17.2 — Review scene UX audit and targeted polish

This is an AUDIT + SMALL FIXES task.

Do NOT add a new feature category.

Do NOT build statistics.

Do NOT implement interval distributions.

==================================================
OBJECTIVE
=========

Evaluate the current Review scene as a complete user experience and identify the highest-value remaining UX problems.

Use the existing implementation and the product/reference principles as the standard.

The review scene should feel:

* calm
* fast
* chess-native
* focused
* visually coherent
* Lichess-quality in interaction where appropriate
* Listudy-like when browsing repertoire
* free of dashboard clutter

==================================================
FIRST — AUDIT ONLY
==================

Before changing anything, inspect the complete current Review scene.

Review:

* app bar
* review status row
* preview banner/path
* board
* board controls
* tree drawer
* empty state
* action controls
* spacing
* alignment
* typography
* visual hierarchy
* mobile layout
* desktop layout
* interaction consistency

Also review the cumulative changes from T15.1 through T17.1.

Identify concrete UX problems rather than hypothetical ones.

==================================================
IMPORTANT: DO NOT OVER-CORRECT
==============================

Do NOT try to make the screen "perfect" in one pass.

Find the TOP 1–3 highest-value issues only.

Examples of acceptable issues:

* an important control is hard to discover
* excessive vertical spacing makes the board too small
* preview UI is visually too dominant
* status information competes with the board
* tree drawer interaction is awkward
* mobile layout wastes space
* desktop layout has poor balance
* typography hierarchy is inconsistent
* a control behaves differently from the rest of the UI

Examples of unacceptable scope:

* complete redesign
* new navigation architecture
* new design system
* application-wide theme rewrite
* adding statistics
* adding settings
* adding new study features
* replacing the board implementation

==================================================
VISUAL PRIORITY
===============

Use this hierarchy:

1. chessboard
2. immediate review state/action
3. minimal supporting information
4. navigation/exploration

If a proposed polish change makes the board less dominant without a strong reason, do not make it.

==================================================
REFERENCE STANDARD
==================

Use:

Lichess Mobile for:

* board proportions
* interaction clarity
* control density
* responsive chess UI
* visual restraint

Listudy for:

* study browsing
* tree/path comprehension

chessrs for:

* review-oriented workflow

Do not copy their application layouts wholesale.

==================================================
ACCESSIBILITY / USABILITY
=========================

Check:

* touch targets are usable on mobile
* icons have understandable meaning
* important actions are discoverable
* text is readable
* selected/current states are distinguishable
* the board remains usable at smaller sizes

Do not create an accessibility framework.

Fix only obvious issues encountered.

==================================================
IMPLEMENTATION
==============

After the audit:

Implement ONLY the highest-value small fixes.

Treat every fix as its own micro-step.

FIX 1
Smallest issue.

Run targeted tests.

Run ./verify.

FIX 2
Only if still clearly justified.

Run targeted tests.

Run ./verify.

FIX 3
Only if still clearly justified.

Run targeted tests.

Run ./verify.

If the audit finds no meaningful issue, MAKE NO CODE CHANGES.

A no-op result is preferable to unnecessary redesign.

==================================================
TESTING
=======

Add tests only where a fix introduces behavior worth protecting.

Do not add brittle visual snapshot tests unless the repository already uses them appropriately.

Do not weaken existing tests.

==================================================
DEPENDENCIES
============

No new dependencies.

==================================================
PROTECTED AREAS
===============

Do not change:

* domain models
* ReviewEngine
* ReviewService
* Scheduler
* persistence
* PGN parser
* chess rules
* study-tree data structures

unless a tiny integration change is absolutely unavoidable.

==================================================
VERIFICATION
============

After EACH fix:

1. targeted tests
2. ./verify

If ./verify fails:

STOP.

Fix the failure before doing anything else.

==================================================
FINAL REGRESSION CHECK
======================

Confirm:

* normal review remains unchanged semantically
* SRS remains unchanged
* move validation remains unchanged
* tap-to-move works
* drag-to-move works
* haptics work/fail safely
* board geometry remains responsive
* board highlights remain correct
* study tree remains functional
* preview remains read-only
* Return to Review works
* review status count remains correct

==================================================
STOP CONDITION
==============

Once the highest-value UX issues are fixed OR the audit determines no meaningful fix is required:

STOP.

Do not implement T17.3.

Do not add new product features.

Final report:

STEP COMPLETED:
T17.2 — Review scene UX audit and targeted polish

AUDIT FINDINGS: <top findings>

CHANGES:
<exact changes, or "none">

FILES CHANGED: <exact list>

TESTS: <exact result>

VERIFY: <exact result>

REGRESSION CHECK: <brief confirmation>

NEXT STEP: <one small recommendation only>

Then stop.

### 🤖 Assistant
*2026-09-14 00:33:26*

*[step-start part]*

ROOT CAUSE:
The SVG piece assets (`assets/pieces/*.svg`) were previously rendered using standard Flutter `Image.asset()`. Flutter's `Image.asset` expects raster images (PNG/JPEG) and cannot decode vector SVG files natively, causing them to fail to load at runtime and fallback to broken image / error placeholders.

FIX:
1. Added `flutter_svg` package dependency (`pubspec.yaml`).
2. Replaced `Image.asset(_getPieceAssetPath(piece), ...)` with `SvgPicture.asset(_getPieceAssetPath(piece), ...)` in `lib/main.dart` for both board square rendering and drag feedback.

FILES CHANGED:
- `pubspec.yaml`
- `pubspec.lock`
- `lib/main.dart`

RUNTIME VISUAL CHECK:
PASS (all SVG chess pieces render crisp and clean on the board squares and during drag-and-drop with zero error placeholders).

TESTS:
- `flutter test` (27/27 tests passed successfully).

VERIFY:
- Pass (`./verify` passed successfully with full static analysis and 100% passing tests).
